with
\delta\eta=Y
;

(iii)

\xi\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\xi=X
;

(iv)

representations
Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}}))
in
[Y]
; and

(v)

representations
(X,\dot{Y})\in\ker(D^{Y}_{1})
of
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}
.

(i)
The choice of
A
:

Let
A^{\prime}=\{A^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))
with
\delta A^{\prime}=\omega
.
Then,
A-A^{\prime}=(A(z)-A^{\prime}(z))dz
is a smooth
1
-form on
M_{0}
and

\displaystyle\iint_{M_{0}}\left((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz-\iint_{M_{0}}\left((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=\iint_{M_{0}}\overline{\partial}(A_{i}-A^{\prime}_{i})d\overline{z}\wedge dz=\iint_{M_{0}}d(A_{i}dz-A^{\prime}_{i}dz)=0

from the Stokes theorem.

(ii)
The choice of
\eta
:

Let
\eta^{\prime}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with

\delta\eta^{\prime}=Y
. Then
H=\eta^{\prime}-\eta\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
. Since
\mu=\overline{\partial}\xi\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
and
H\mu q=H(z)\mu(z)q(z)d\overline{z}
is a global
(0,1)
-form on
M_{0}
,

\displaystyle\iint_{M_{0}}\left((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}(\mu q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz-\iint_{M_{0}}\left((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}^{\prime}}(\mu q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=\iint_{M_{0}}L_{H}(\mu q)d\overline{z}\wedge dz=\iint_{M_{0}}(H\mu q)_{z}(z)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=-\iint_{M_{0}}d\left((H\mu q)d\overline{z}\right)=0.

(iii)
The choice of
\xi
:

Let
\xi^{\prime}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\delta\xi^{\prime}=X
. Take
A^{\prime}=\{A^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))
satisfying

A^{\prime}_{j}dz-A^{\prime}_{i}dz=(X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi^{\prime}_{j}q))dz

for
i
,
j\in I
. Then,
\Xi=\xi^{\prime}-\xi
defines a smooth vector field on
M_{0}
.
Let
\nu=\overline{\partial}\eta\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
. Define
B=\{B_{i}dz\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))
by

B_{i}dz=(A^{\prime}_{i}-A_{i}-L_{\eta_{i}}(\Xi q))dz.

Then,

\displaystyle(\delta B)_{ij}

\displaystyle=B_{j}dz-B_{i}dz

\displaystyle=L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi^{\prime}_{j}q)dz-L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)dz-(L_{\eta_{j}}(\Xi q)-L_{\eta_{i}}(\Xi q))dz

\displaystyle=L_{Y_{ij}}(\Xi q)dz-L_{\eta_{j}-\eta_{i}}(\Xi q)dz=0.

Hence,
B
defines a global
1
-form on
M_{0}
. Notice that

\displaystyle d(L_{\eta_{i}}(\Xi q)dz)

\displaystyle=d((\eta_{i}\Xi q)_{z}dz)=((\eta_{i}\Xi q)_{z\overline{z}})d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=(-(\nu\Xi q)_{z}+(\eta_{i}\Xi_{\overline{z}}q)_{z})d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=(-(\nu\Xi q)_{z}+L_{\eta_{i}}(\Xi_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle d(B_{i}dz)=d((A^{\prime}_{i}-A_{i}-L_{\eta_{i}}(\Xi q))dz)

\displaystyle=((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-(A_{i})_{\overline{z}}+(\nu\Xi q)_{z}-L_{\eta_{i}}(\Xi_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=\left((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz-\left((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz+(\nu\Xi q)_{z}d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=\left((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz-\left((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz-d((\nu\Xi q)d\overline{z})

for
i\in I
.
This means that

\displaystyle\iint_{M_{0}}\left((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz-\iint_{M_{0}}\left((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=\iint_{M_{0}}d(B_{i}dz+(\nu\Xi q)d\overline{z})=0.

(iv) The choice of
Y
in
[Y]
:

Take
A\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
and the cocycle
\omega
for
Y
as the statement.
Let
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. From
Proposition

5.3.2
and (
5.5.2
), for
Y+\delta\beta
,
the representatives of
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
and
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
are
(X,\dot{Y}+K^{\prime}(\beta,X))
and
(\psi+L_{\beta}(q),q)
respectively. Hence, from (
5.2.4
) and (
5.2.5
), the cocycle
\omega^{\prime}
which we consider here should be

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}_{ij}dz

\displaystyle=X_{ij}(\psi_{i}+L_{\beta_{i}}(q))dz-(\dot{Y}_{ji}+[\beta_{i},X_{ji}])qdz+L_{Y_{ij}+\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}}(\xi_{j}q)dz

\displaystyle=(X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)+X_{ij}L_{\beta_{i}}(q)-[\beta_{i},X_{ji}]q+L_{\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}}(\xi_{j}q))dz

\displaystyle=(X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)+L_{\beta_{j}}(\xi_{j}q)-L_{\beta_{i}}(\xi_{i}q))dz.

From (i) and (ii), we may take
A^{\prime}=\{A^{\prime}_{i}dz\}_{i\in I}=\{A_{i}dz+L_{\beta_{i}}(\xi_{i}q)dz\}_{i\in I}
. In this case, the integrand becomes

\displaystyle((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}+\beta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=((A_{i}+L_{\beta_{i}}(\xi_{i}q))_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}+\beta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz,

which implies the integral does not change.

(v) The choice of
(X,\dot{Y})
in
\ker(D^{Y}_{1})
:

Take
A\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
and the cocycle
\omega
for
Y
as the statement.
Let
\alpha
,
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. We consider
(X^{\prime},\dot{Y}^{\prime})=(X,\dot{Y})+D_{0}^{Y}(\alpha,\beta)
. In this case, the cocycle
\omega^{\prime}
which we consider here is

\displaystyle\omega^{\prime}_{ij}dz

\displaystyle=(X_{ij}+\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})\psi_{i}dz-(\dot{Y}_{ji}+\beta_{i}-\beta_{j}+[\alpha_{j},Y_{ji}])qdz+L_{Y_{ij}}((\xi_{j}+\alpha_{j})q)dz

\displaystyle=\left(X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)+(\alpha_{j}\psi_{j}-\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}+\beta_{i}q-\beta_{j}q\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\alpha_{j}L_{Y_{ij}}(q)-[\alpha_{j},Y_{ij}]qdz+L_{Y_{ij}}(\alpha_{j}q)\right)dz

\displaystyle=\left(X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)\right)dz+(\alpha_{j}\psi_{j}-\beta_{j}q)dz-(\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}-\beta_{i}q)dz

from (
5.2.5
). Therefore, from (i) and (ii), we may take
A^{\prime}=\{A^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}
defined by

A_{i}^{\prime}dz=A_{i}dz+(\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}-\beta_{i}q)dz

for
i\in I
. Then,
\delta A^{\prime}=\omega^{\prime}
and
the integrand satisfies

\displaystyle((A^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{j}+\alpha_{j})_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz=(A_{i}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

and the integral does not change.
∎

Definition 8

(Model of the pairing)
.

Let
[Y]\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
The
model of the pairing

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\colon\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]\oplus\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]\to\mathbb{C}

is defined by

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left((X,\dot{Y}),(\psi,q)\right).

Notice in the calculation that we first fix a representative
Y
in
[Y]
, and take representatives
(X,\dot{Y})\in\ker(D^{Y}_{1})
of
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
and
(\psi,q)\in\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
of
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
.

We claim the non-degeneracy of the model of the pairing. It can be also deduced from the presentation of the model of the pairing under the trivialization (cf.
Theorem

6.4.1
).

Proposition 5.5.1
.

When the locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
satisfies
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
,
the model of the pairing
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}
is non-degenerate.

Proof.

Let
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
. Suppose

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=0

for all
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}^{\dagger}[\mathcal{U}]
.
We will conclude
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}=0
.

Let
(X,\dot{Y})\in\ker(D^{Y}_{1})
be a representative of
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
and a representative
(\psi,q)\in\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
of
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
. We take
\xi
,
\eta
,
A
, and
\omega
as
Lemma

5.5.1
.

When
q=0
, from the definition,
\psi=\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})
and

(5.5.5)

\omega_{ij}=X_{ij}\psi

for
i
,
j\in I
.
This means that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,0\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(A_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=-\pi{\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\psi\}_{i,j\in I}])

(cf. §
3.3.3
). Since the residue defines a non-degenerate pairing,
we have
[X]=0
in
H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Therefore, there is
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
with
\delta\alpha=X
. In this case,
we may assume
\xi=\alpha
from (3) of
Lemma

5.5.1
. From (
5.2.5
) and (
5.3.8
),

(5.5.6)

\displaystyle\omega_{ij}

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\alpha_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+\alpha_{j}L_{Y_{ij}}(q)-[\alpha_{j},Y_{ij}]q

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}=\alpha_{j}(\psi_{i}+L_{Y_{ij}}(q))-\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}-(\dot{Y}_{ji}-[\alpha_{j},Y_{ji}])q}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{j}\psi_{j}-\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}-(\dot{Y}_{ji}-[\alpha_{j},Y_{ji}])q

\displaystyle=\alpha_{j}\psi_{j}-\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}+(\dot{Y}_{ij}-[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}])q.

Notice from
Proposition

5.3.1
,
\dot{Y}-K(\alpha,Y)=\{\dot{Y}_{ij}-[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}]\}_{i,j\in I}\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Therefore,
in this case, the pairing satisfies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem\delta\alpha,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(A_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=-\pi{\rm Res}([\{(\dot{Y}_{ij}-[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}])q\}_{i,j\in I}]).

Since
(\psi,q)
is taken arbitrary in
\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
, we have

\dot{Y}-K(\alpha,Y)=\delta\beta
for some
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Thus, we obtain

(X,\dot{Y})=(\delta\alpha,\delta\beta+K(\alpha,Y))=D^{Y}_{0}(\alpha,\beta),

and
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}=0
.

Next, let
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
.
Suppose that

\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=0

for all
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
.

As the above discussion, we fix
(X,\dot{Y})\in\ker(D^{Y}_{1})
be a representative of
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
and a representative
(\psi,q)\in\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
of
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
, and take
\xi
,
\eta
,
A
, and
\omega
as
Lemma

5.5.1
.

Suppose first that
[X]=0
. Then, there is
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
such that
\delta\alpha=X
. From the discussion around (
5.5.6
), the cocycle
\omega
satisfies

\displaystyle\omega_{ij}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{j}\psi_{j}-\alpha_{i}\psi_{i}+(\dot{Y}_{ij}-[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}])q.

Hence,

0=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem\delta\alpha,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=-\pi{\rm Res}([(\dot{Y}_{ij}-[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}])q]).

Since
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
, from
Proposition

5.3.1
,
the totality of
[(\dot{Y}_{ij}-[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}]
fills
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})=H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Therefore, the non-degeneracy of the residue implies that
q=0
.
Thus, from the discussion around (
5.5.5
),
\psi
satisfies

0=\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem\delta\alpha,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,0\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)=-\pi{\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\psi_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}]).

From the non-degeneracy of the residue again, we obtain
\psi=0
. Thus, we conclude
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}=0
.
∎

5.5.2.
Dolbeaut presentation and Pairing

We claim

Lemma 5.5.2
.

Let
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a locally finite covering of
M_{0}
with
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
.
Let
(X,\dot{Y})\in\ker(D^{Y}_{1})
and
(\psi,q)\in\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
.
Let
\xi=\{\xi_{i}\}_{i\in I}
,
\dot{\eta}=\{\dot{\eta}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\delta\xi=X
and
\dot{Y}=\delta\dot{\eta}+K(\xi,Y)
. Then

(\xi_{j}\psi_{j}+\dot{\eta}_{j}q)-(\xi_{i}\psi_{i}+\dot{\eta}_{i}q)=X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

Proof.

Indeed, from
(
5.2.4
) and (
5.2.5
),

\displaystyle X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\xi_{j}-\xi_{i})\psi_{i}-(\dot{\eta}_{i}-\dot{\eta}_{j}+[\xi_{j},Y_{ji}])q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

\displaystyle=\xi_{j}\psi_{i}-\xi_{i}\psi_{i}-\dot{\eta}_{i}q+\dot{\eta}_{j}q-[\xi_{j},Y_{ji}]q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

\displaystyle=\xi_{j}(\psi_{j}-L_{Y_{ij}}(q))-\xi_{i}\psi_{i}-\dot{\eta}_{i}q+\dot{\eta}_{j}q+[\xi_{j},Y_{ij}]q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\xi_{j}\psi_{j}+\dot{\eta}_{j}q)-(\xi_{i}\psi_{i}+\dot{\eta}_{i}q){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\xi_{j}L_{Y_{ij}}(q)+[\xi_{j},Y_{ij}]q}+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\xi_{j}\psi_{j}+\dot{\eta}_{j}q)-(\xi_{i}\psi_{i}+\dot{\eta}_{i}q){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-L_{\xi_{j}}(Y_{ij}q)}+L_{Y_{ij}}(\xi_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\xi_{j}\psi_{j}+\dot{\eta}_{j}q)-(\xi_{i}\psi_{i}+\dot{\eta}_{i}q),

which implies what we wanted.
∎

Theorem 5.5.1

(Dolbeaut presentation and Pairing)
.

Let
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a locally finite covering of
M_{0}
with
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
.
Let
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\in\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]
and
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]
. Let
\eta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\delta\eta=Y
and

\mathscr{D}_{0}^{-1}(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}})=\left\langlebar\mu,\dot{\nu}\right\ranglebar_{{\scriptscriptstyle\eta}}\in\mathbb{T}_{\eta}[\mathcal{U}].

Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}((\mu\psi_{i}+\dot{\nu}_{i}q)-L_{\eta_{i}}(\mu q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz.

Proof.

Under the notation in
Lemma

5.5.2
,
let
\mu=-\overline{\partial}\xi_{i}
and
\dot{\nu}=-\overline{\partial}\dot{\eta}_{i}
.
When we set
A_{i}dz=(\xi_{i}\psi_{i}+\dot{\eta}_{i}q)dz
,

((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}-L_{\eta_{i}}((\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz=-(\mu\psi_{i}+\dot{\nu}q-L_{\eta_{i}}(\mu q))d\overline{z}\wedge dz.

Hence, the formula follows from the definition of the pairing.
∎

5.6.
Model space of
T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
be the vector bundle of holomorphic quadratic differentials over
\mathcal{T}_{g}
. Let
\Pi_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}\colon\mathcal{Q}_{g}\to\mathcal{T}_{g}
is the projection. As discussed in §
3.2
, the bundle
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
is canonically identified with the cotangent bundle
T^{*}\mathcal{T}_{g}
over
\mathcal{T}_{g}
. In this section, we review Hubbard and Masur’s description of the holomorphic tangent space to
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
given in
[
28
]
.

5.6.1.
Tangent space

The tangent space
T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
of
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
at a holomorphic quadratic differential
q_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}=H^{1}(M_{0},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})
is described as the first hyper-cohomology group of the complex of sheaves
\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}}
:

(5.6.1)

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>\Theta_{M_{0}}@>{L_{\cdot}(q_{0})}>{}>\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}@>{}>{}>0,\end{CD}

where
L{\cdot}(q_{0})
is the Lie derivative along a vector field given at (
5.2.2
)
(cf.
[
28
, Proposition 3.1]
).

Fix a locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
M_{0}
. Then, the tangent space
T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
is the first cohomology group
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
of the following double complex:

(5.6.2)

\begin{CD}0\\
@A{}A{}A\\
C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{\delta}>{}>C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})\\
@A{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L_{\cdot}q_{0}}}A{}A@A{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-L_{\cdot}q_{0}}}A{}A\\
C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{\delta}>{}>C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{\delta}>{}>C^{2}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}}).\end{CD}

Then,

\displaystyle{\bf Z}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=\{(X,\varphi)\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega^{\otimes 2}_{M_{0}})\mid\delta\varphi-L_{X}(q_{0})=0\}

\displaystyle{\bf B}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=\{(\delta\alpha,\{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L_{\alpha_{i}}(q_{0})}\}_{i},)\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega^{\otimes 2}_{M_{0}})\mid\alpha=\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\}

and

{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})={\bf Z}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})/{\bf B}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}}).

The first
hyper-cohomology group

{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
is defined by taking the direct limit of
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
in terms of the refinement of (locally finite) coverings. We denote by
[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
the cohomology class of
(X,\varphi)\in{\bf Z}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
.

The following proposition might be well-known. Indeed, Hubbard and Masur deal with the case of the direct limits in
[
28
, Proposition 4.5]
. However, they do not indicate conditions of the covering in the statement (but, it is implicitly given). Hence, we give a proof for completeness and for confirmation, although the idea here is the same as that given by Hubbard and Masur.

Proposition 5.6.1
.

When a locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
satisfies that
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for all
i\in I
,

{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
is isomorphic to
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
. In particular,
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
is isomorphic to
T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
.

Proof.

We consider the following commutative diagram:

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>0@>{}>{}>C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{id}>{}>C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{}V{}V@V{\delta\oplus{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L_{\cdot}(q_{0})}}V{}V@V{}V{\delta_{2}}V\\
0@>{}>{}>C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{inc}>{}>C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{pr_{1}}>{}>C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{}>{}>0\\
@V{\delta_{1}}V{}V@V{\delta_{2}\oplus(\delta_{1}-L_{\cdot}(q_{0}))}V{}V@V{}V{\delta_{2}}V\\
0@>{}>{}>C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{inc}>{}>>C^{2}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{pr_{1}}>{}>C^{2}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{}>{}>0,\end{CD}

where, in the above diagram,
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
are coboundary map for cochains of sheaves
\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\oplus 2}
and
\Theta_{M_{0}}
,
inc
means the inclusion to the second coordinate, and
pr_{1}
is the projection on the first coordinate
(cf. the diagram in
[
28
, p.264]
). This diagram leads the exact sequence

\begin{CD}H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{}>{}>H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{}>{}>{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})@>{}>{}>H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{}>{}>H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}).\end{CD}

Since
M_{0}
does not admit a (global) holomorphic vector field, we have
H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
. Let
(q_{0})
be the divisor of
q_{0}
. The sheaf
\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}
is naturally isomorphic to the sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}}((q_{0}))
of meromorphic functions on
M_{0}
which is multiples of the divisor
(q_{0})
, where a section on
U\subset M_{0}
is a meromorphic function on
U
satisfying
(f)+D\geq 0
(e.g.
[
19
, §16.4, §17.4]
).
From the Serre duality,

H^{1}(M_{0},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})\cong H^{1}(M_{0},\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}}((q_{0})))\cong H^{0}(M_{0},\Omega_{M_{0}}(-(q_{0})))^{*}.

An element in
H^{0}(M_{0},\Omega_{M_{0}}(-(q_{0}))
is a holomorphic
1
-form
\omega
on
M_{0}
with
(\omega)-(q_{0})\geq 0
, and
2g-2=\deg((\omega))\geq\deg((q_{0}))=4g-4
. Therefore,
H^{1}(M_{0},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})=0
if
g\geq 2
.
Since the canonical map
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})\to H^{1}(M_{0},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})
is injective, we conclude that
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})=0
.
Thus, when
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for all
i\in I
, we have the following exact sequence

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}\cong H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{}>{}>{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})@>{}>{}>H_{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\cong T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}@>{}>{}>0,\end{CD}

which canonically represents the fibration

Q_{M_{0}}\to T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}\to T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
via the isomorphism
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\cong T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
(cf.
[
28
, Proposition 4.5]
).
∎

Remark 5.6.1
.

From the above discussion, the map

T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}\cong{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\ni[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}\to[X]\in H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\cong T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}

is the horizontal projection (cf. §
2.5
), which
presents the differential

D\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{T}_{g}}=D\Pi_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}|_{q_{0}}\colon T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}\to T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}

of the projection
\Pi^{\dagger}_{\mathcal{T}_{g}}=\Pi_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}\colon T^{*}\mathcal{T}_{g}=\mathcal{Q}_{g}\to\mathcal{T}_{g}
, where
x_{0}=\Pi_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}(q_{0})
. As discussed in the proof of
Proposition

5.6.1
, the kernel, which is nothong but the vertical space, is isomorphic to
T^{*}_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\cong H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})
.

5.6.2.
Description with holomorphic families

Let
B
be a neighborhood of the origin in
\mathbb{C}
.
Let
(\mathcal{M},\pi,B)
be a holomorphic family of compact Riemann surfaces of genus
g
. For
t\in B
, set
M_{t}=\pi^{-1}(t)
. By taking
B
sufficiently small, we identify
\mathcal{M}
with the product
M_{0}\times B
via the local trivialization as
C^{\infty}
-manifolds (cf.
[
43
, Theorem 2.4]
). By the implicit mapping theorem, when
B
is taken sufficiently small if necessary again, there is a covering
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of
M_{0}
and an injective holomorphic map
Z_{i}\colon U_{i}\times B_{0}\to\mathbb{C}\times B_{0}
such that
z_{i}^{t}=Z_{i}\mid_{U_{i}\times\{t\}}\colon U_{i}\times\{t\}\to\mathbb{C}
(
i\in I
) make an analytic chart of
M_{t}
for all
t\in B
. We identify
U_{i}\times\{t\}
with
U_{i}
and define
z_{ij}^{t}=z^{t}_{i}\circ(z_{j}^{t})^{-1}
on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
.
Let
\{q_{t}\}_{t\in B}
be a holomorphic family of holomorphic quadratic differentials with
q_{t}\in M_{t}
. Suppose that
q_{t}
is presented as
q_{t}^{t}(z)dz^{2}
on
z^{0}_{i}(U_{i})
. Set

\displaystyle X_{ij}(z)

\displaystyle=\left.\dfrac{\partial z_{ij}^{t}}{\partial t}\right\mid_{t=0}\circ(z_{ij}^{0})^{-1}(z)\quad(z\in z_{i}^{0}(U_{i}\cap U_{j}))

\displaystyle\varphi_{i}(z)

\displaystyle=\left.\dfrac{\partial q_{i}^{t}}{\partial t}\right|_{t=0}(z)\quad(z\in z_{i}^{0}(U_{i}))

(cf. §
8.1.2
).
Then,
(X,\varphi)=(\{X_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I},\{\varphi_{i}\}_{i\in I})\in{\bf Z}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
and it represents the tangent vector of the family
\{q_{t}\}_{t\in B}
at
t=0
. Indeed, since

z_{ij}^{t}(z)=z_{ij}^{0}(z)+tX_{ij}(z_{ij}(z))+o(t),

for
z\in z_{j}(U_{i}\cap U_{j})
,

\displaystyle q_{i}^{t}(z_{ij}^{t}(z))\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{t}}{dz}(z)^{2}

\displaystyle=q_{i}^{t}\left(z_{ij}^{0}(z)+tX_{ij}(z_{ij}^{0}(z))+o(t)\right)\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{0}}{dz}(z)^{2}\left(1+tX_{ij}^{\prime}(z_{ij}^{0}(z))\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{0}}{dz}(z)+o(t)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=q_{0}(z_{ij}^{0}(z))\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{0}}{dz}(z)^{2}+t\left(\varphi_{i}(z_{ij}^{0}(z))+L_{X_{ij}}(q_{0})(z_{ij}^{0}(z))\right)\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{0}}{dz}(z)^{2}+o(t)

\displaystyle q_{j}^{t}(z)

\displaystyle=q_{0}(z)+t\varphi_{j}(z)+o(t)

as
t\to 0
. This means that

\varphi_{j}(z)-\varphi_{i}(z_{ij}^{0}(z))\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{0}}{dz}(z)^{2}=L_{X_{ij}}(q_{0})(z_{ij}^{0}(z))\dfrac{dz_{ij}^{0}}{dz}(z)^{2}

for
z\in z_{j}(U_{i}\cap U_{j})
, which is written as

\varphi_{j}-\varphi_{i}=L_{X_{ij}}(q_{0})\quad(i,j\in I)

or
\delta\varphi=L_{X}(q_{0})

for short.

5.6.3.
Tangent spaces to strata

The following is essentially due to Hubbard-Masur
[
28
]
and Dumas
[
15
]
.
For the sake of completeness, we give a brief proof.

Proposition 5.6.2

(Tangent space to the strata)
.

Let
q_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{g}(k_{1},\cdots,k_{n};\epsilon)
.
Let
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a locally finite covering of
M_{0}
such that each
U_{i}
contains at most
1
zero of
q_{0}
, and
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
.
A tangent vector
[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\cong T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
is in
T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}(k_{1},\cdots,k_{n};\epsilon)
, then for any
i\in I
, a meromorphic function
\varphi_{i}/q_{0}
has at most simple poles.

Proof.

The following argument is a modification of that given by Dumas
[
15
, Lemma 5.2]
.

As we discussed in §
5.6.2
, each
\varphi_{i}
is appeared by differentiating a holomorphic family
\{q_{t}\}_{|t|<\epsilon}
of holomorphic quadratic differentials on a local chart
(U_{i},z_{i})
. For the simplicity of the argument, we may assume that
z^{t}_{i}(z_{0})=0
and
q_{0}=z^{k}dz^{2}
on
z_{i}(U_{i})
, where
k
is the order of zero at
z_{0}
of
q_{0}
.
Since
q_{t}
has the same symbol as
q_{0}
, we can write

q_{t}=\alpha_{t}^{*}(z^{k}dz^{2})

for
|t|<\epsilon
and some holomorphic function
\alpha_{t}
which depends holomorphically in
t
and
\alpha_{0}(z)=z
.
Then,

\varphi_{i}=z^{k-1}(k\alpha^{\prime}+2z\dot{\alpha}^{\prime})dz^{2}

and
\varphi_{i}
has a zero of order at least
k-1
at
z_{0}
. Hence
\varphi_{i}/q_{0}
has at most simple poles on
U_{i}
.
∎

5.7.
The model space of
T^{*}_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
and Models of the pairing and the holomorphic symplectic form

5.7.1.
Model space

We set

{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})=\{[\Phi,Y]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}}\mid[-Y,\Phi]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\}.

and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
as its direct limit. We will adopt the space
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
as the
model space
of the cotangent space
T^{*}_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
to
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
at
q_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{g}
. A representative
(\Phi,Y)
of
[\Phi,Y]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
satisfies

\Phi_{j}-\Phi_{i}=-L_{Y_{ij}}(q_{0})

for
i,j\in I
.

5.7.2.
Model of pairing

Let
\mathcal{U}
be a locally finite covering of
M_{0}
. We define the
pairing
between
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
by

(5.7.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}([X,\varphi]_{q_{0}},[\Phi,Y]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}\left((\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))+(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi_{i}-L_{\xi_{i}}(q_{0}))\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz,

where
\xi
,
\eta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\delta\xi=X
and
\delta\eta=Y
.
We check the pairing (
5.7.1
) is well-defined.

We first check that the integral in (
5.7.1
) is independent of the choices of
\xi
and
\eta
.
Let
\xi^{\prime}=\{\xi^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\delta\xi^{\prime}=X
. Then,
\Xi=\xi_{i}-\xi^{\prime}_{i}
on
U_{i}
defines a global vector field on
M_{0}
. The difference between the integrals in the right-hand side (
5.7.1
) defined from
\xi
and
\xi^{\prime}
is equal to

\displaystyle\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}\left(\Xi_{\overline{z}}(\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))-(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}L_{\Xi}(q_{0})\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}d(\Xi(\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))dz+2(q_{0}\Xi(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}})d\overline{z})=0,

since the one form

\Xi(\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))dz+2q_{0}\Xi(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}d\overline{z}

in the integral is the global one form on
M_{0}
.
The well-definedness for
\eta
is treated by the same argument.

We next check that the integral in (
5.7.1
) is independent of the choices of the cocycles.
The cohomology class for the cocycle
(X,\varphi)
consists of
(X+\delta\alpha,\varphi+L_{\alpha}(q_{0}))

for
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
Let
\xi^{\prime}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))

with
\delta\xi^{\prime}=X+\delta\alpha
. Since
\delta(\xi^{\prime}-\alpha)=X
, from the previous discussion, the integrand in the right-hand side of (
5.7.1
) is

\displaystyle(\xi^{\prime}_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))+(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi_{i}+L_{\alpha_{i}}(q_{0})-L_{\xi^{\prime}_{i}}(q_{0}))

\displaystyle(\xi^{\prime}_{i}-\alpha_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))+(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi_{i}-L_{\xi^{\prime}_{i}-\alpha_{i}}(q_{0}))

\displaystyle=(\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\Phi_{i}+L_{\eta_{i}}(q_{0}))+(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi_{i}-L_{\xi_{i}}(q_{0})).

The case for
[\Phi,Y]\in{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
follows from the same argument.

For two locally finite coverings
\mathcal{U}
,
\mathcal{V}
.
Suppose that
\mathcal{V}
is a refinement of
\mathcal{U}
.
Since the natural maps
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\to{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{V},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\to{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{V},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
are defined by the restriction, for the pairing (
5.7.1
) descends to the pairing on
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\times{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
.

Proposition 5.7.1

(Non-degenerate)
.

When a locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
satisfies
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
, the pairing (
5.7.1
) is non-degenerate.

Proof.

Let
[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
.
Suppose that

\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}([X,\phi]_{q_{0}},[\Phi,Y]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}})=0

for all
[\Phi,Y]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
.
Let
\Psi\in\mathcal{Q}_{g}
. Since
[\Psi,0]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
,

0=\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}([X,\phi],[\Psi,0])=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(\xi_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)\Psi(z)d\overline{z}\wedge dz.

From the assumption of the covering,
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\cong H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
Therefore,
[X]=0
in
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

(cf. §
3.3.2
).
Therefore, there is
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
such that
\delta\alpha=X
. We may adopt
\alpha
as
\xi
in the formula (
5.7.1
) of the pairing and

0=\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}([\delta\alpha,\varphi]_{q_{0}},[\Phi,Y]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}})=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(\eta_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi_{i}(z)-L_{\alpha_{i}}(q_{0})(z))d\overline{z}\wedge dz.

Since
\{\varphi_{i}-L_{\alpha_{i}}(q_{0})\}_{i\in I}
defines a (global) holomorphic quadratic differential on
M_{0}
, we get
\varphi_{i}-L_{\alpha_{i}}(q_{0})=0
for all
i
, since the pairing (
3.1.1
) is non-degenerate.
Thus, we get
[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}=[\delta\alpha,L_{\alpha}(q_{0})]^{\dagger}_{q_{0}}=0
.
The remaining case is dealt with the same argument.
∎

5.7.3.
Kawai’s description of the holomorphic symplectic form

In this section, following Kawai
[
34
]
, we recall the holomorphic symplectic form on the tangent space of the space of holomorphic quadratic differentials,
and rewrite Kawai’s symplectic form under our setting.

First we recall Kawai’s description.
Let
M_{0}
be a closed Riemann surface of genus
g
.
Let
q_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}

Let
\Gamma_{0}
be a cocompact Fuchsian group acting on the upper-half plane
\mathbb{H}
with
\mathbb{H}/\Gamma_{0}=M_{0}
. In the following discussion, we identify differentials on a Riemann surfaces with automorphic forms on its universal covering space.

Let
\mu_{j}
(
j=1
,
\cdots
,
3g-3
) be a system of smooth
(-1,1)
-automorphic forms whose Teichmüller equivalence classes span the tangent space at
x_{0}=(M_{0},f_{0})\in\mathcal{T}_{g}
.
In
[
34
]
, Kawai takes such a basis from the space of harmonic Beltrami differentials.
We can check that his calculation is valid for a system of
3g-3
smooth
(-1,1)
-forms which form a basis on the tangent space to the Teichmüller space at
x_{0}
.

Let
q_{s}^{\alpha}
(
\alpha=1,2
,
|s|<\epsilon
) be a holomorphic family of quadratic differentials with
q_{0}^{1}=q_{0}^{2}=q_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}
. Suppose
q_{s}^{\alpha}\in\mathcal{Q}_{x^{\alpha}_{s}}
and
x^{\alpha}_{s}=(M^{\alpha}_{s},f^{\alpha}_{s})
with
x^{1}_{0}=x^{2}_{0}=x_{0}
. Suppose that the Beltrami differential of
f^{\alpha}_{s}\colon M_{0}\to M^{\alpha}_{s}
is equal to
\nu_{\alpha}(s)=\sum_{j=1}^{3g-3}\epsilon^{\alpha}_{j}(s)\mu_{j}
for some holomorphic functions
\epsilon^{\alpha}_{j}
(
\alpha=1
,
2
and
j=1
,
\cdots
,
3g-3
).
Let
w^{\nu_{\alpha}(s)}
be the normalized quasiconformal mapping on
\mathbb{H}
satisfying the Beltrami equation
(w^{\nu_{\alpha}(s)})_{\overline{z}}=\nu_{\alpha}(s)(w^{\mu^{i}(s)})_{z}
,
w^{\nu_{\alpha}(s)}(0)=w^{\nu_{\alpha}(s)}(1)-1=0
and
w^{\mu_{\alpha}(s)}(\infty)=\infty
.
Then, Kawai describes the holomorphic symplectic form on
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
as

(5.7.2)

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}(t_{1},t_{2})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{3g-3}\left\{\left(\iint_{\mathbb{H}/\Gamma_{0}}[\dot{q}^{1}_{0}(z)+(q^{1}_{0})^{\prime}(z)\dot{w}^{\nu_{1}(0)}(z)+2q_{0}^{1}(z)(\dot{w}^{\nu_{1}(0)})_{z}(z)]\mu_{j}(z)dxdy\right)\dot{\epsilon}^{2}_{j}(0)\right.

\displaystyle\quad-\left.\left(\iint_{\mathbb{H}/\Gamma_{0}}[\dot{q}^{2}_{0}(z)+(q^{2}_{0})^{\prime}(z)\dot{w}^{\nu_{2}(0)}(z)+2q_{0}^{2}(z)(\dot{w}^{\nu_{2}(0)})_{z}(z)]\mu_{j}(z)dxdy\right)\dot{\epsilon}^{1}_{j}(0)\right\},

where
t_{\alpha}\in T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
is tangent to the family
\{q^{\alpha}_{s}\}_{|s|<1}
at
s=0
.
See
[
34
, (3.4)]
.

We claim

Proposition 5.7.2

(Holomorphic symplectic form)
.

Fix a locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{\alpha}\}_{i\in I}
with
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
.
The holomorphic symplectic form
\omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}
on
\mathcal{Q}_{g}
is described on the model space
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
of
T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
by

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}([X^{1},\varphi^{1}]_{q_{0}},[X^{2},\varphi^{2}]_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{4i}\left(\iint_{M_{0}}(\xi^{1}_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi^{2}_{i}-L_{\xi^{2}_{i}}(q_{0}))-\iint_{M_{0}}(\xi^{2}_{i})_{\overline{z}}(\varphi^{1}_{i}-L_{\xi^{1}_{i}}(q_{0}))\right)d\overline{z}\wedge dz

for
[X^{\alpha},\varphi^{\alpha}]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
and
\xi^{\alpha}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
[\delta\xi^{\alpha}]=[X^{\alpha}]
(
\alpha=1
,
2
).

Proof.

As we mentioned above, we rewrite Kawai’s holomorphic symplectic form under our setting.
Fix a locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
.
Suppose that for
\alpha=1,2
,
t_{\alpha}=[X^{\alpha},\varphi^{\alpha}]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\cong T_{q_{0}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
.
The restriction
\xi^{\alpha}_{i}
of the minus of the derivative
-\dot{w}^{\nu_{i}(0)}
(in Kawai’s formula) to
U_{i}
defines a cochain
\xi^{\alpha}=\{\xi^{\alpha}_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
[\delta\xi^{\alpha}]=[X^{\alpha}]
(cf. §
13.1
).
Notice that

(\xi^{\alpha})_{\overline{z}}=-\nu_{i}(0)|_{U_{i}}=-\sum_{j=1}^{3g-3}\epsilon^{\alpha}_{j}(0)\mu_{j}|_{U_{i}}

for
\alpha=1
,
2
and
i\in I
.
From (
5.7.2
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}(t_{1},t_{2})

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\left(-\iint_{M_{0}}[\varphi^{1}_{i}(z)-L_{\xi^{1}_{i}}(q_{0})(z)](\xi^{2}_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)dxdy\right.

\displaystyle\qquad+\left.\iint_{M_{0}}[\varphi^{2}_{i}(z)-L_{\xi^{2}_{i}}(q_{0})(z)](\xi^{1}_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)dxdy\right),

which implies what we wanted.
∎

5.8.
Remarks on regularity of cochains in the formulae of pairings

All
0
-chains in the formulae of the pairings in
Lemma

5.5.1
,
Theorem

5.5.1
, §
5.7.2
, and
Proposition

5.7.2
are assumed to be smooth. However, for the application, we may need to think of such
0
-chains with weaker regularity conditions.

Henceforth, let
\mathscr{C}^{k,s}(D)
be the function space consisting of continuous functions all of whose
s
-th derivarives are in
L^{s}
for all
s\leq k
. By definition,
\mathscr{C}^{0,s}(D)=L^{s}(D)
.

For a Riemann surface
M
and a , let
\mathscr{C}^{k,s:p,q}(\mathcal{S})
be the sheaf of germs of
(p,q)
-forms of class
\mathscr{C}^{k,s}
with coefficients in
\mathcal{S}=\Theta_{M}
or
\Omega_{M}
.
It is known that the Green formula holds for differentials of class
\mathscr{C}^{1,1}
. For instance, see
[
47
, p.150 (6.17)]
.
Threfore, in
Lemma

5.5.1
and
Theorem

5.5.1
, we can check that the proofs of the formulae work with

•

\xi
,
\eta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{2,1:0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}})
,

•

A\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{1,1:0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))
,

•

\mu\in L^{\infty}_{(-1,1)}(M_{0})

with
-\overline{\partial}\xi=\mu
for some
\xi\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{2,1:0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
,

•

\dot{\nu}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{0,\infty:0,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))

with
-\overline{\partial}\dot{\eta}=\dot{\nu}
for some
\dot{\eta}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{2,1:0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
which satisfies

\delta\dot{\eta}+K(\xi,\delta\eta)=0.

For the formulae of the pairing and the symplectic form in
Lemma

5.5.1
and
Theorem

5.5.1
, we can also check that the proofs work with
\xi
,
\eta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{2,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
.

Indeed, the invariance of the pairing represented with Dolbeaut presentations in
Theorem

5.5.1
follows from the discussion in
Lemma

5.5.1
for

A_{i}=\xi_{i}\psi_{i}+\dot{\eta}_{i}q.

The other case can be similarly checked.

In general, we need such smoothness of cochains to the validity of the formulae and the invariance in terms of the representatives, since we apply the Green theorem in checking the invariance. However, in some case, the formulae also valid with the slightly weaker assumption.

Indeed, the Dolbeaut presentations in
Theorem

5.5.1
also holds with cochains
\xi
and
\eta
such that
-\overline{\partial}\xi
and
-\overline{\partial}\eta
are Teichmüller Beltrami differentials (cf. §
3.2.3
). A Teichmüller Beltrami differential on
M_{0}
, by definition, forms
\overline{\varphi}/|\varphi|
for some
\varphi\in\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}
. Hence, it is real analytic except for the zeros of
\varphi
, but not continuous on whole
M_{0}
, which means that such
\xi
and
\eta
are not in
C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathscr{C}^{2,1:0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
However, in general, for a holomorphic function
f
on a domain
D
,

\left(\dfrac{\overline{f}}{|f|}\right)_{z}=-\dfrac{(\overline{f})^{2}}{2|f|^{3}}f^{\prime},\quad\left(\dfrac{\overline{f}}{|f|}\right)_{\overline{z}}=\dfrac{\overline{f^{\prime}}}{2|f|}\in L^{1}_{loc}(D)

since zeros of a holomorphic function are discrete.
Hence, for any relatively compact simply conneted subdomain
D^{\prime}
in
D
, we can construct
\eta\in\mathscr{C}^{2,1}(D^{\prime}-{\rm Zero}(f))
such that
\eta_{\overline{z}}=-\overline{f}/|f|\in L^{\infty}(D)
(cf.
[
32
, Proposition 4.19]
). The discussion with the Green theorem works for such differentials
by applying the Royden-type argument given in
[
68
]
(cf. §
11.1.1
). Namely, we first take a smooth exhaustion of the complement of the zeros of the quadratic differntials (which defines the Teichmüller Beltrami differential), apply the Green theorem for such domain in the exhaustion, and take the limit.

Chapter 6
Trivializations of Models of Spaces and Pairings

6.1.
Guiding frame, Good section for the coboundary operator

Henceforth, for discussing trivializations, we fix a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map, which we call a
guiding frame
,

\mathfrak{gf}\colon H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to L_{(-1,1)}^{\infty}(M_{0})
such that

(i)

for
X\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,
\mathfrak{gf}([X])
represents
[X]\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\cong H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\cong T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
. Namely
\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X])=[\mathfrak{gf}([X])]
in
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
; and

(ii)

for
[X]\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,
each
\mathfrak{gf}([X])
is a smooth Beltrami differential. Namely,
\mathfrak{gf}([X])\in\Gamma(M_{0},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
.

Example 6.1.1

(Ahlfors-Weill guiding frame)
.

Since the Ahlfors-Weill section
\mathscr{A}_{x_{0}}
is
\mathbb{C}
-linear, the differential
D\mathscr{A}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}
from
T_{0}\mathcal{T}^{B}_{x_{0}}=B_{2}(\mathbb{D}^{*},\Gamma_{0})
to
T_{0}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma_{0})=L^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma_{0})
coincides with
\mathscr{A}_{x_{0}}
. Hence,

(6.1.1)

\mathfrak{gf}=\mathscr{A}_{x_{0}}\circ D\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\circ\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}\colon H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to L^{\infty}(\mathbb{D},\Gamma_{0})\cong L_{(-1,1)}^{\infty}(M_{0})

is a guiding frame, where
D\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\colon T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\to T_{0}\mathcal{T}^{B}_{x_{0}}=B_{2}(\mathbb{D}^{*},\Gamma_{0})
is the differential of the Bers embedding
\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}
at
x_{0}\in\mathcal{T}_{g}
(cf. §
3.1.3
).
We call such guiding frame the
Ahlfors-Weill guiding frame
.

Indeed, let
[X]\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. each
\mathfrak{gf}([X])
is a smooth Beltrami differential. Since

\displaystyle[\mathfrak{gf}([X])]

\displaystyle=\left.D\mathscr{P}^{B}_{x_{0}}\right|_{0}\circ\mathfrak{gf}([X])=\left.D\mathscr{P}^{B}_{x_{0}}\right|_{0}\circ\mathscr{A}_{x_{0}}\circ D\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\circ\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X])

\displaystyle=D\left.(\mathscr{P}^{B}_{x_{0}}\circ\mathscr{A}_{x_{0}}\right)_{0}\circ D\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\circ\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X])

\displaystyle=(D\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}})^{-1}\circ D\mathscr{B}_{x_{0}}|_{x_{0}}\circ\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X])=\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X])

from (
3.1.4
),

\mathfrak{gf}([X])
represents
[X]
.

Proposition 6.1.1

(Good section for
\delta
)
.

For a guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}
, there is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map
\mathfrak{L}=\mathfrak{L}^{\mathfrak{gf}}
from
Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
to
C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
such that

(a)

\delta\circ\mathfrak{L}(X)=X
for
X\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
; and

(b)

\mathfrak{L}\circ\delta(\alpha)=\alpha
for
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.

Especially, the linear map
\mathfrak{L}
is uniquely determined from the guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}
with the above condition (a) and the following condition (c):

(c)

\overline{\partial}\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}=-\mathfrak{gf}([X])
on
U_{i}
for
i\in I
.

Actually, the condition (b) follows from the conditions (a) and (c).

Proof.

Let
X=\{X_{ij}\}_{i,j}\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. For an index
j
, we take
\xi_{j}\in\Gamma(U_{j},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
which satisfies
\overline{\partial}\xi_{j}=-\mathfrak{gf}([X])
on
U_{i}
. By the assumption, there is an
\{\alpha_{j}\}_{j}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
such that

(6.1.2)

\xi_{j}-\xi_{i}=X_{ij}+(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})

on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}

for all indices
i
,
j\in I
(cf. §
3.3
).

We claim that the difference
\xi_{j}-\alpha_{j}\in\Gamma(U_{j},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
is independent of the choice of
\xi_{j}
’s. Indeed, take
\xi^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma(U_{j},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{j}\in\Gamma(U_{j},\Theta_{M_{0}})
satisfying
\overline{\partial}\xi^{\prime}_{j}=-\mathfrak{gf}([X])
on
U_{i}
and

\xi^{\prime}_{j}-\xi^{\prime}_{i}=X_{ij}+(\alpha^{\prime}_{j}-\alpha^{\prime}_{i})

on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
for all
i
,
j\in I
.
Then,

\xi^{\prime}_{j}-\xi^{\prime}_{i}-(\alpha^{\prime}_{j}-\alpha^{\prime}_{i})=X_{ij}=\xi_{j}-\xi_{i}+(\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i})

and

(\xi_{j}-\alpha_{j})-(\xi^{\prime}_{j}-\alpha^{\prime}_{j})=(\xi_{i}-\alpha_{i})-(\xi^{\prime}_{i}-\alpha^{\prime}_{i})

on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
for all
i
,
j
. Since
\overline{\partial}\xi_{j}=\overline{\partial}\xi^{\prime}_{j}=-\mathfrak{gf}([X])\mid_{U_{j}}
,

\xi=(\xi_{j}-\alpha_{j})-(\xi^{\prime}_{j}-\alpha^{\prime}_{j})

on
U_{j}
defines a holomorphic vector field on
M
. Since
\chi(M)<0
,
M
has no non-trivial holomorphic vector field. Therefore

\xi_{j}-\alpha_{j}=\xi^{\prime}_{j}-\alpha^{\prime}_{j}

for all
j
, and we confirm the claim.

Let us return to the proof of
Proposition

6.1.1
.
We define a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map
\mathfrak{L}\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
by

\mathfrak{L}(X)=\{\xi_{j}-\alpha_{j}\}_{j}.

We check that
\mathfrak{L}
satisfies the desired conditions. Indeed, from (
6.1.2
),
\mathfrak{L}
satisfies the condition (a).
We check that the condition (b) follows from the conditions (a) and (c). Let
\alpha=\{\alpha_{j}\}_{j}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Since
\mathfrak{gf}([\delta\alpha])=\mathfrak{gf}(0)=0
, the condition (c) implies that
\mathfrak{L}(\delta\alpha)_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})
for each
i\in I
. From the condition (a),
\delta\circ\mathfrak{L}(\delta\alpha)=\delta\alpha
. Hence

\xi=\mathfrak{L}(\delta\alpha)_{i}-\alpha_{i}

on
U_{i}
defines a holomorphic vector field on
M_{0}
, and
\mathfrak{L}(\delta\alpha)=\alpha
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{L}
satisfies the condition (b).

We check the uniqueness. Suppose
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}
satisfies the conditions (a) and (c).
Consider
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime\prime}\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
defined by
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime\prime}(X)=\mathfrak{L}(X)-\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(X)
. From the condition (c),
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime\prime}(X)_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})
for
i\in I
. From the condition (a),

\delta\circ\mathfrak{L}^{\prime\prime}(X)=\delta\circ\mathfrak{L}(X)-\delta\circ\mathfrak{L}^{\prime}(X)=X-X=0.

Hence
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime\prime}(X)
defines a global holomorphic vector field on
M_{0}
. Hence
\mathfrak{L}^{\prime\prime}(X)=0
for all
X\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
∎

As (
5.3.1
), we have

(6.1.3)

\delta(S(\mathfrak{L}(X),Y))=\zeta(X,Y)

for
X,Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.

6.2.
Trivialization of model spaces of
T_{[Y]}T\mathcal{T}_{g}

In the following three sections, we assume that a locally finite covering
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
satisfies
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for all
i\in I
. In this section, we will show that the model spaces
\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]
and
\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
defined in
Definitions

1
and
3
are isomorphic to the product space
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}\cong H^{1}(M_{0},\mathcal{O}_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}
. In particular, in this case,

(6.2.1)

\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]=6g-6

for
[Y]\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
(cf.
Theorem

6.2.1
).

Let

\Pi\colon C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})/\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

be the projection. Since
\delta\circ\delta=0
, the coboundary operator
\delta\colon C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to Z^{2}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

descends to a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map

\delta\colon C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})/\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to Z^{2}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}}).

The existence in the following lemma is trivial since
H^{2}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
.
We give and fix a concrete construction of the coboundary.

Lemma 6.2.1

(Coboundary of the primary obstraction)
.

There is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map

E\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}\to C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})/\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

such that

(a)

\delta\circ E(X,Y)=\zeta(X,Y)
for
X,Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
;

(b)

For
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,
\Pi\circ S(\alpha,Y)=E(\delta\alpha,Y)
; and

(c)

For
X\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
and
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,

K^{\prime}(\beta,X)+\dfrac{1}{2}[X,\delta\beta]-E(X,\delta\beta)=0

modulo
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.

(d)

For
X,Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,

E(X,Y)=E(Y,X)
in
C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})/\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{L}\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
be a linear map defined in
Proposition

6.1.1
.
Then

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}S(\mathfrak{L}(X),Y)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\overline{\partial}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},Y_{ij}]

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}[\overline{\partial}\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}+\overline{\partial}\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},Y_{ij}]

\displaystyle=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),Y_{ij}]

\displaystyle=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}].

Take
\sigma_{j}\in\Gamma(U_{j},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with

\overline{\partial}\sigma_{i}=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X])\mid_{U_{i}},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]
on
U_{i}
. We define

(6.2.2)

E_{ij}(X,Y)=S(\mathfrak{L}(X),Y)_{ij}-(\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{i})\in\Gamma(U_{i}\cap U_{j},\Theta_{M_{0}})

for
X
,
Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Then,
E(X,Y)=\{E_{ij}(X,Y)\}_{i,j}
is determined up to coboundaries in
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
(which is caused only from the choice of
\sigma=\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
).
From the linearity of
\mathfrak{L}
,
E
defines a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map

E\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}\to C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})/\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}}).

We check that the map
E
satisfies the desired conditions.
Indeed, from (
6.1.3
),

\delta\circ E(X,Y)=\delta\circ T(X,Y)=\delta\circ S(\mathfrak{L}(X),Y)=\zeta(X,Y).

Hence
E
satisfies the condition (a).

Let
\alpha=\{\alpha_{j}\}_{j}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Since
\mathfrak{gf}([\delta\alpha])=\mathfrak{gf}(0)=0
and
\overline{\partial}\sigma_{j}=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]=0
on
U_{j}
, the vector field
\sigma_{j}
in (
6.2.2
) is a holomorphic vector field on
U_{j}
for all
j
. From
Proposition

6.1.1
and (
6.2.2
),

E(\delta\alpha,Y)\equiv T(\delta\alpha,Y)=S(\mathfrak{L}(\delta\alpha),Y)=S(\alpha,Y)

modulo
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Therefore,
E
satisfies the condition (b).

We check (c). Indeed, from the definition of
E
and (b) of
Proposition

6.1.1
,

(6.2.3)

E_{ij}(X,\delta\beta)=S(\mathfrak{L}(X),\delta\beta)_{ij}-(\sigma^{\prime}_{j}-\sigma^{\prime}_{i})

where
\overline{\partial}\sigma^{\prime}_{j}=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\beta_{j}]
on
U_{j}
.
By (a) of
Proposition

6.1.1
,

\displaystyle K^{\prime}(\beta,X)_{ij}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X,\delta\beta]_{ij}-S(\mathfrak{L}(X),\delta\beta)_{ij}+(\sigma^{\prime}_{j}-\sigma^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle=[\beta_{j},\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}]+\dfrac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\dfrac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}]+(\sigma^{\prime}_{j}-\sigma^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle=([\beta_{j},\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}]+\sigma^{\prime}_{j})-([\beta_{i},\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}]+\sigma^{\prime}_{i}).

Since
[\beta_{i},\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}]+\sigma^{\prime}_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})
for
i\in I
,
we have done.

Finally we check (d).
Let
\sigma
,
\sigma^{\prime}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
such that
\overline{\partial}\sigma_{i}=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]
and

\overline{\partial}\sigma^{\prime}_{i}=-[\mathfrak{gf}([Y]),\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}]
on
U_{i}
.
From the definition (
6.2.2
) of
E
,

\displaystyle E_{ij}(X,Y)-E_{ij}(Y,X)

\displaystyle=S(\mathfrak{L}(X),Y)-S(\mathfrak{L}(Y),X)-(\sigma_{j}-\sigma^{\prime}_{j})+(\sigma_{i}-\sigma^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle=([\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]-(\sigma_{j}-\sigma^{\prime}_{j}))

\displaystyle\quad-([\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]-(\sigma_{i}-\sigma^{\prime}_{i})).

Since for
i\in I
,

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}([\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]-(\sigma_{i}-\sigma^{\prime}_{i}))

\displaystyle=-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]-[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{gf}([Y])]

\displaystyle\quad-(-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]+[\mathfrak{gf}([Y]),\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}])=0,

E(X,Y)-E(Y,X)\equiv 0
modulo
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
∎

We claim the following:

Theorem 6.2.1

(Trivialization of model spaces)
.

Let
E
be the map defined in
Lemma

6.2.1
.
Then, the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map
\mathscr{I}_{Y}\colon\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]\to H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}

defined by

(6.2.4)

\mathscr{I}_{Y}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\right)=\left([X],\left[\dot{Y}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X,Y]-E(X,Y)\right]\right)

is an isomorphism which satisfies

(6.2.5)

\mathscr{I}_{Y+\delta\beta}\circ\mathcal{L}_{\beta;Y}=\mathscr{I}_{Y}

for all
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
In particular, the isomorphism
\mathscr{I}_{Y}
descends to the isomorphism

\mathscr{I}_{[Y]}\colon\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]\to H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}.

Proof.

We first check that the map (
6.2.1
) is well-defined. From
Lemma

6.2.1
, the cochain in the bracket in the second coordinate of the map (
6.2.1
) is cocycle. We check that the restriction of the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map
Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
defined by

(X,\dot{Y})\mapsto\left([X],\left[\dot{Y}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X,Y]-E(X,Y)\right]\right)

to the image of
D^{Y}_{0}
(defined in (
5.3.7
)) is trivial. Let
\alpha
,
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. The first coordinate of the image of
D^{Y}_{0}(\alpha,\beta)
under the above map is trivial because of the definition of the cohomology group
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
For the second coordinate,

\displaystyle\left(\delta\beta+K(\alpha,Y)+\dfrac{1}{2}[\delta\alpha,Y]-E(\delta\alpha,Y)\right)_{ij}

\displaystyle\equiv\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}+[\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}]+\dfrac{1}{2}[\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}]-\dfrac{1}{2}[\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{j},Y_{ij}]=\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}\equiv 0

modulo
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. Hence, (
6.2.4
) is well-defined.

We construct the inverse of the map (
6.2.1
). For
X
,
W\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
, we define

\dot{W}=W-\dfrac{1}{2}[X,Y]+E(X,Y).

Then, from
Lemma

6.2.1
,

\displaystyle\delta\left(\dot{W}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X,Y]\right)-\zeta(X,Y)

\displaystyle=\delta\left(W+E(X,Y)\right)-\zeta(X,Y)=0.

Hence we have a well-defined
\mathbb{C}
-linear map

(6.2.6)

(X,W)\mapsto\left\lsem X,W-\dfrac{1}{2}[X,Y]+E(X,Y)\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}

from
Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
to
\mathbb{T}_{Y}
. We check that the image of the direct sum of two copies of
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
under (
6.2.6
) is trivial. Let
\alpha
,
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
. When we substitute
\delta\alpha
and
\delta\beta
to
X
and
W
in (
6.2.6
), the
ij
-component of the second coordinate is equal to

\displaystyle\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}-\dfrac{1}{2}[\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i},Y_{ij}]+E(\delta\alpha,Y)_{ij}

\displaystyle\equiv\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}-\dfrac{1}{2}[\alpha_{j}-\alpha_{i},Y]+\dfrac{1}{2}[\alpha_{i}+\alpha_{j},Y_{ij}]=\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}+K(\alpha,Y)_{ij}

modulo
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,
which implies that the image of
(\delta\alpha,\delta\beta)
under the map (
6.2.6
) is zero in
\mathbb{T}_{Y}
.
Hence, the map (
6.2.6
) descends to the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map
from
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}
to
\mathbb{T}_{Y}
. From the definition, this map is the inverse of (
6.2.1
).

Equation

6.2.5
follows from (c) of
Lemma

6.2.1
.
∎

6.2.1.
Trivialization for Dolbeault type presentation

We continue to use the linear maps
\mathfrak{gf}
,
\mathfrak{L}
and
E
in
Proposition

6.1.1
and
Lemma

6.2.1
.
Let
Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
We define a
\mathbb{C}
-linear map

\tilde{\mathscr{I}^{D}}_{Y}\colon\ker(D^{Y,1}_{0})\to\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))^{\oplus 2}
by

(6.2.7)

\tilde{\mathscr{I}^{D}_{Y}}(\mu,\dot{\nu})=(\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\hat{\nu})

where
[X]\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
with
[\mathfrak{gf}([X])]=[\mu]
(cf. (
3.2.1
)) and the differential
\hat{\nu}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
in (
6.2.7
) is defined by

(6.2.8)

\hat{\nu}=(\dot{\nu}_{i}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}])+[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]

on
U_{i}
where
\chi\in\Gamma(M_{0},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with
\overline{\partial}\chi=\mathfrak{gf}([X])-\mu
.
Since

\displaystyle[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]-[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]+[-\chi,Y_{ij}]=0

on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
,
from
Proposition

5.4.1
,

(\mu,\dot{\nu})
and
(\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\{\dot{\nu}_{i}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]\}_{i\in I})
are
equivalent in
\mathbb{T}^{Dol}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]
.
We claim

Theorem 6.2.2

(Trivialization for Dolbeault type presentation)
.

The map
\tilde{\mathscr{I}^{D}_{Y}}
descends to a
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphism

\mathscr{I}^{D}_{Y}\,\colon\,\mathbb{T}^{Dol}_{Y}\to(T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g})^{\oplus 2}

which commutes the following diagram

(6.2.9)

\begin{CD}\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]@<{\mathscr{D}_{0}}<{}<\mathbb{T}^{Dol}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]\\
@V{\mathscr{I}_{Y}}V{}V@V{}V{\mathscr{I}^{D}_{Y}}V\\
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}@>{\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}\oplus\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}}>{}>(T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g})^{\oplus 2},\end{CD}

where
\mathscr{D}_{0}
is the connecting homomorphism defined at (
5.4.3
), and
\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}
is the isomorphism defined at (
3.3.3
). Furthermore, in the commutative diagram (
6.2.9
),
the vertical spaces satisfy

(6.2.10)

\begin{CD}\mathbb{T}^{V}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]@<{\left.\mathscr{D}_{0}\right|_{\mathbb{T}^{Dol,V}_{Y}}}<{}<\mathbb{T}^{Dol,V}[\mathcal{U}]_{Y}\\
@V{\left.\mathscr{I}_{Y}\right|_{\mathbb{T}_{Y}^{V}}=\{[0]\}\oplus\mathbf{vi}^{\mathbb{T}}_{\scriptscriptstyle Y}:\mathcal{U}}V{}V@V{}V{\left.\mathscr{I}^{D}_{Y}\right|_{\mathbb{T}^{Dol}_{Y}}}V\\
\{[0]\}\oplus H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})@>{\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}\oplus\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}}>{}>\{[0]\}\oplus T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}.\end{CD}

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}[\chi,Y_{ij}]=[\overline{\partial}\chi,Y_{ij}]

\displaystyle=[\mathfrak{gf}([X])-\mu,Y_{ij}]

on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
,
we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\dot{\nu}_{j}-\dot{\nu}_{i}+[\mu,Y_{ij}]

\displaystyle=(\dot{\nu}_{j}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}])-(\dot{\nu}_{i}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}])+[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),Y_{ij}]

on
U_{i}\cap U_{j}
for
i
,
j\in I
.
Let
\beta\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
with

-\overline{\partial}\beta_{i}=\dot{\nu}_{i}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]
on
U_{i}
,
and
\dot{Y}=\delta\beta+K(\mathfrak{L}(X),Y)
.
Then,

\mathscr{D}_{0}\left(\left\langlebar\mu,\dot{\nu}\right\ranglebar^{\scriptscriptstyle cycl}_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\right)=\mathscr{D}_{0}\left(\left\langlebar\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\{\dot{\nu}_{i}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]\}_{i\in I}\right\ranglebar^{\scriptscriptstyle cycl}_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\right)=\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}

from the definition of the connecting homomorphism.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle\dot{Y}_{ij}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X_{ij},Y_{ij}]-E(X,Y)_{ij}

\displaystyle=\beta_{j}-\beta_{i}+[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},Y_{ij}]+\dfrac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]

\displaystyle\quad-\dfrac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]+\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{i}

\displaystyle=(\beta_{j}+\sigma_{j})-(\beta_{i}+\sigma_{i})

modulo
\delta C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
,
where
\sigma_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
satisfies
-\overline{\partial}\sigma_{i}=[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]
(cf. (
6.2.2
)).
Since

-\overline{\partial}(\beta_{i}+\sigma_{i})=(\dot{\nu}_{i}-\overline{\partial}[\chi,\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}])+[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]=\hat{\nu}_{i}

on
U_{i}
,

\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}\left(\left[\dot{Y}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X,Y]-E(X,Y)\right]\right)=[\hat{\nu}]\in T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}.

As a consequence,

(\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}\oplus\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}})\circ\mathscr{I}_{Y}\circ\mathscr{D}_{0}\left(\left\langlebar\mu,\dot{\nu}\right\ranglebar^{\scriptscriptstyle cycl}_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\right)=([\mu],[\hat{\nu}])
, which
is nothing but the equivalence class of
\tilde{\mathscr{I}^{D}}_{Y}(\mu,\dot{\nu})

in
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\oplus T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
.

The commutative diagram (
6.2.10
) follows from the diagram (
6.2.9
), the definition of
E
((
5.3.2
), (
6.2.3
)), the definitions of the vertical spaces (
Definitions

2
and
5
),
and
Proposition

5.4.2
.
∎

Remark 6.2.1
.

The map
E
defined in
Lemma

6.2.1
are dependent on the choices of the linear map
\mathfrak{gf}\colon H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to L_{(-1,1)}^{\infty}(M_{0})
.
Therefore, so are the trivializations in
Theorems

6.2.1
and
6.2.2
.

6.3.
Trivialization of the model space of
T^{*}_{[Y]}T\mathcal{T}_{g}

Fix a guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}\colon H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to L_{(-1,1)}^{\infty}(M_{0})
and take
\mathfrak{L}\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{0})\to C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
defined in
Proposition

6.1.1
.

Let
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
.
Let
Y\in Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
and
(\psi,q)=(\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in I},q)\in\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
be representatives of
[Y]
and
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
respectively.
Since

\psi_{j}-\psi_{i}=L_{Y_{ij}}(q)=L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}}(q)-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)

for
i
,
j\in I
,
\psi-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)}(q)=\{\psi_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)\}_{i\in I}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}))
.

The exact sequence

\begin{CD}0@>{}>{}>\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}@>{inc}>{}>\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{\overline{\partial}}>{}>\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{}>{}>0\end{CD}

leads an exact sequence

(6.3.1)

\begin{CD}H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})@>{}>{}>H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}))@>{\overline{\partial}}>{}>H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}))\\
@>{}>{}>H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}).\end{CD}

Since
\delta L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)}(q)=L_{Y}(q)\in C^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})
,
\overline{\partial}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)}(q)\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,1}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}))
.
Since
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2})=0
,
there is a unique smooth quadratic differential
Q=\{Q_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}))
which satisfies

(6.3.2)

\overline{\partial}Q_{i}=\overline{\partial}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)

for
i\in I
,
and the
uniqueness condition

(6.3.3)

\iint_{M_{0}}\mathfrak{gf}([Z])Q=\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}\mathfrak{gf}([Z])(z)Q(z)d\overline{z}\wedge dz=0

for all
[Z]\in H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
The uniqueness of such
Q
follows from the fact that
\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}
is the dual of
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
and

T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\cong H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\ni[Z]\mapsto\iint_{M_{0}}\mathfrak{gf}([Z])Q\in\mathbb{C}

is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear functional.
We claim:

Theorem 6.3.1

(Trivialization)
.

Under the above notation,

(6.3.4)

\mathscr{I}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}\colon\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]\ni\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\mapsto(\{\psi_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+Q\}_{i\in I},q)\in\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}

is a
\mathbb{C}
-isomorphism.

Proof.

Since
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]=2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}=6g-6
,
it suffices to show that the map (
6.3.4
) is injective.

Suppose that
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
is in the kernel of the map (
6.3.4
).
Let
(\psi,q)\in\ker(D^{Y,\dagger}_{1})
be the representative of
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}
.
From the second coordinate, we have
q=0
. From (
6.3.2
),
\overline{\partial}Q=\overline{\partial}\psi=0
. Hence,
Q
is holomorphic.
However, from the uniqueness condition (
6.3.3
), we have
Q=0
,
and hence
\psi=0
. As a conclusion, we obtain
\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}=0
.
∎

6.4.
Presentations of the pairings under trivializations

Let
x_{0}=(M_{0},f_{0})\in\mathcal{T}_{g}
and
[Y]\in H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\cong T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
.
Let
\mathcal{U}=\{U_{i}\}_{i\in I}
be a locally finite covering of
M_{0}
with
H^{1}(U_{i},\Theta_{M_{0}})=0
for
i\in I
.
Fix a guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}\colon H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to L^{\infty}_{(-1,1)}(M_{0})

and the good section
\mathfrak{L}\colon Z^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Theta_{M_{0}}))
in
Proposition

6.1.1
.
Consider the trivializations
\mathscr{I}_{[Y]}
on
\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]

in
Theorem

6.2.1

and

\mathscr{I}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}
on
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]

in
Theorem

6.3.1

defined from the guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}
and the good section
\mathfrak{L}
.

Let
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\in\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]
and

\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
.
Let
(X,\dot{Y})
and
(\psi,q)
be the representatives.
We continue to use the notation in §
6.2
and §
6.3
frequently.
In the following discussion, we notice that though
E(X,Y)
is determined up to
C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
, the ambiguity does not affect in the calculation.

For
i,j\in I
,

\displaystyle X_{ij}(\psi_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+Q)+\left(\dot{Y}_{ij}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X_{ij},Y_{ij}]-E(X,Y)_{ij}\right)q

\displaystyle=X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

\displaystyle\quad-X_{ij}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+X_{ij}Q-\dfrac{1}{2}[X_{ij},Y_{ij}]q-E(X,Y)_{ij}q-L_{Y_{ij}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

and the last five terms become

\displaystyle-X_{ij}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+X_{ij}Q-\dfrac{1}{2}[X_{ij},Y_{ij}]q-E(X,Y)_{ij}q-L_{Y_{ij}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

\displaystyle=-(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i})L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i})Q

\displaystyle\quad-\dfrac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]q-\dfrac{1}{2}[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]q

\displaystyle\quad+(\sigma_{j}-\sigma_{i})q-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}-\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

\displaystyle=-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}Q-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}Q

\displaystyle\quad-[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]q+[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]q

\displaystyle\quad+(\sigma_{j}q-\sigma_{i}q-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}Q+\sigma_{j}q-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}}(q))-(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}Q+\sigma_{i}q-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q))

\displaystyle\quad-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]q+L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}}(q)-[\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j},\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}]q-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

\displaystyle=(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}Q+\sigma_{j}q-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{j}}(q))-(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}Q+\sigma_{i}q-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q))

from (
5.2.5
)
since
Q
and
q
are global sections on
M_{0}
.
Hence, for
\Omega=\{\Omega_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))
with

\Omega_{j}-\Omega_{i}=X_{ij}(\psi_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+Q)+\left(\dot{Y}_{ij}+\dfrac{1}{2}[X_{ij},Y_{ij}]-E(X,Y)_{ij}\right)q,

we define
A=\{A_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))

by

A_{i}=\Omega_{i}+(-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}Q-\sigma_{i}q+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)).

on
U_{i}
.
Then,
A=\{A_{i}\}_{i\in I}
satisfies

A_{j}-A_{i}=X_{ij}\psi_{i}-\dot{Y}_{ji}q+L_{Y_{ij}}(\mathfrak{L}(X)_{j}q)

and

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}A_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}((\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}\Omega_{i}+\mathfrak{gf}([X])Q-\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}\overline{\partial}(L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q))-[\mathfrak{gf}([X]),\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}]]q

\displaystyle\quad+\mathfrak{gf}([X])L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q)+\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}\overline{\partial}(L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q))-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(\mathfrak{gf}([X])q)

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}\Omega_{i}+\mathfrak{gf}([X])Q.

from (
5.2.9
).
Therefore, we deduce

\displaystyle-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}((A_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)-L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}((\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i})_{\overline{z}}q)(z))d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}((\Omega_{i})_{\overline{z}}(z)+\mathfrak{gf}([X])(z)Q(z))d\overline{z}\wedge dz

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}d\Omega_{i}

from the uniqueness condition (
6.3.3
) of
Q
.
Thus,
from (
3.3.7
), we obtain the following.

Theorem 6.4.1

(Trivialization and Pairing)
.

Under the above notation,
for
\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\in\mathbb{T}_{Y}[\mathcal{U}]
and

\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
,
we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{[Y]}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle Y}}\right)

\displaystyle=([X],[Z])\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})^{\oplus 2}

\displaystyle(\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}\oplus\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}})(([X],[Z]))

\displaystyle=([\mu],[\hat{\nu}])\in T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}\oplus T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}\left(\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)

\displaystyle=(\varphi,q)\in\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}.

Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}\left(\left\lsem X,\dot{Y}\right\rsem_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}},\left\lsem\psi,q\right\rsem^{\dagger}_{{\scriptscriptstyle[Y]}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\pi\left({\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\varphi\}_{i,j\in I}])+{\rm Res}([\{Z_{ij}q\}_{i,j\in I}])\right)

\displaystyle=\langle[\mu],\varphi\rangle+\langle[\hat{\nu}],q\rangle.

6.5.
Trivialization of the model space of
T_{q_{0}}T^{*}\mathcal{T}_{g}
and
T^{*}_{q_{0}}T^{*}\mathcal{T}_{g}
, and the Pairing under the trivializations

As well as the previous sections,
we fix a guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}\colon H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})\to L_{(-1,1)}^{\infty}(M_{0})
,
and take
\mathfrak{L}
as
Proposition

6.1.1
.
For
[X,\varphi]\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})

and
[\Phi,Y]\in{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
, we take
Q
,

Q^{\prime}\in H^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}^{\otimes 2}))
satisfying

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}Q_{i}

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}(\varphi-L_{\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}}(q_{0}))=\overline{\partial}(L_{\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}}(q_{0}))

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}Q^{\prime}_{i}

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}(\Phi_{i}+L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q_{0}))=-\overline{\partial}(L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q_{0}))

on
U_{i}

and

\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}\mathfrak{gf}([Z])Qd\overline{z}\wedge dz=\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}\mathfrak{gf}([Z])Q^{\prime}d\overline{z}\wedge dz=0

for all
[Z]\in H^{1}(M_{0},\Theta_{M_{0}})
as §
6.3
.
We claim

Theorem 6.5.1

(Trivialization)
.

Under the above notation,

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*}_{q_{0}}

\displaystyle\colon{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\ni[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}\mapsto([X],\{\varphi_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}}(q_{0})+Q\}_{i\in I})\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*\dagger}_{q_{0}}

\displaystyle\colon{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})\ni[\Phi,Y]_{q_{0}}\mapsto(\Phi+L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)}(q_{0})+Q^{\prime},[Y])\in\mathcal{Q}_{M_{0}}\oplus H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

are
\mathbb{C}
-isomorphisms.

Proof.

We only check that
\mathscr{I}^{*}_{q_{0}}
is
\mathbb{C}
-isomorphic. Since the dimensions of the both sides are same, it suffices to show that the map is injective.
Suppose that
[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}
is in the kernel of the map.
Since
[X]=0
,
X=\delta\alpha
for some
\alpha\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
.
From
Proposition

6.1.1
,
\mathfrak{L}(X)=\mathfrak{L}(\delta\alpha)=\alpha
. Thus,

\overline{\partial}Q=-\overline{\partial}(\psi-L_{\alpha}(q_{0}))=0

and hence
Q=0
and
\varphi=L_{\alpha}(q_{0})
.
Thus, we obtain

[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}=[\delta\alpha,L_{\alpha}(q_{0})]_{q_{0}}=0

and we have done.
∎

We define the pairing
\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}
between models
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})

and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
, and the symplectic form
\omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}
on the model
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})

at (
5.7.1
) and
Proposition

5.7.2
.
We claim

Theorem 6.5.2

(Pairing and Symplectic form under the trivializations)
.

Under the above notation, for
[X,\varphi]_{q_{0}}
,
[X^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})

and
[\Phi,Y]_{q_{0}}\in{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
, we set

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*}_{q_{0}}([X,\varphi]_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=([X],\psi)=(\{[\{X_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}],\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in I})\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*}_{q_{0}}([X^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}]_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=([X^{\prime}],\psi^{\prime})=(\{[\{X^{\prime}_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}],\{\psi^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i\in I})\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}^{*\dagger}_{q_{0}}([\Phi,Y]_{q_{0}})

\displaystyle=(\Psi,[Y])=(\{\Psi_{i}\}_{i\in I},[\{Y_{ij}\}_{i,j\in I}])\in\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}\oplus H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

and

\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X])=[\mu]
,
\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([X^{\prime}])=[\mu^{\prime}]
,

\mathscr{T}_{x_{0}}([Y])=[\nu]\in T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}([X,\varphi],[\Phi,Y])

\displaystyle=-\pi\left({\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\Psi_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}])+{\rm Res}([\{Y_{ij}\psi_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}])\right)

\displaystyle=\langle[\mu],\Psi\rangle+\langle[\nu],\psi\rangle

\displaystyle\omega_{\mathcal{Q}_{g}}([X,\varphi],[X^{\prime},\varphi^{\prime}])

\displaystyle=\dfrac{\pi}{2}\left({\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\psi^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}])-{\rm Res}([\{X^{\prime}_{ij}\psi_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}])\right)

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2}(\langle[\mu],\psi^{\prime}\rangle-\langle[\mu^{\prime}],\psi\rangle).

Proof.

We only check the formula for the pairing. The formula for the symplectic form is also deduced by the similar argument.
We use the notation given above frequently.

We define
\{\Omega_{i}\}_{i\in I}\in C^{0}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{A}^{0,0}(\Omega_{M_{0}}))
by

\Omega_{i}=\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}\Psi_{i}+\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}\psi_{i}.

Then,

\Omega_{j}-\Omega_{i}=X_{ij}\Psi_{i}+Y_{ij}\psi_{i}

and

\displaystyle-\pi\left({\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\Psi_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}])+{\rm Res}([\{Y_{ij}\psi_{i}\}_{i,j\in I}])\right)

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}d\Omega_{i}=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(\mathfrak{gf}([X])\Psi_{i}+\mathfrak{gf}([Y])\psi_{i})

\displaystyle=-\dfrac{1}{2i}\iint_{M_{0}}(\mathfrak{gf}([X])(\Phi_{i}+L_{\mathfrak{L}(Y)_{i}}(q_{0})+Q^{\prime})+\mathfrak{gf}([Y])(\varphi_{i}-L_{\mathfrak{L}(X)_{i}}(q_{0})+Q))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}([X,\varphi],[\Phi,Y])

from the uniqueness condition for
Q
and
Q^{\prime}
.
∎

6.6.
Dualities in model spaces

As we discussed in §
3.2
and §
3.3.3
,
the pairing defined by the residue

H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\times\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}\ni([X],\varphi)\mapsto-\pi{\rm Res}([\{X_{ij}\varphi\}_{i,j\in I}])\in\mathbb{C}

is non-degenerate, and gives a duality between
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

and
\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}
. Under the identification
H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

with the tangent space
T_{x_{0}}\mathcal{T}_{g}
, the duality gives a recognition of
\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}
as the cotangent space
T_{x_{0}}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{g}
.

Let
[Y]\in H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})
and
q_{0}\in\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}
.
As we observed that the models of pairings
\mathcal{P}_{\mathbb{TT}}
between
\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
and
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
, and
\mathcal{P}^{\dagger}
between
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
are non-generate.
Hence, these pairing makes
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
as the dual spaces of
\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
and
{\bf H}^{1}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
, respectively.

Recall from Linear algebra, for vector spaces
V
and
W
, a natural isomorphism
(V\oplus W)^{*}\cong V^{*}\oplus W^{*}
of the dual spaces of the direct sum is induced by the pairing

(V\oplus W)\times(V^{*}\oplus W^{*})\ni((v,w),(v^{*},w^{*}))\mapsto v^{*}(v)+w^{*}(w)

(e.g.
[
16
, II.5, p.90]
).

We now fix a guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}
. The we obtain the isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]

\displaystyle\to H^{1}(\mathcal{U},\Theta_{M_{0}})\oplus\mathcal{Q}_{x_{0}}

via the trivializations defined from the guiding frame
\mathfrak{gf}
.
From
Theorem

6.4.1
and
Theorem

6.5.2
, the trivializations of the model spaces are natural in terms of the pairings.

As a consequence, the trivializations for
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}_{[Y]}[\mathcal{U}]
and
{\bf H}^{1,\dagger}(\mathcal{U},\mathbb{L}_{q_{0}})
defined in
Theorem

6.3.1
and
Theorem

6.5.1
